(5.3)

By omitting the middle line of (
5.3
), we obtain the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{0}}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n-1}}
\textstyle{A_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{n}\alpha_{n}}
\textstyle{A_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{n+1}\alpha_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma f_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{1}}
\textstyle{B_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n-1}}
\textstyle{B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\textstyle{B_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma B_{0}}

We define
f_{k}=\mu_{k}\alpha_{k}
for
2\leq k\leq n+1
, then
(f_{0},f_{1},f_{2},\cdots,f_{n+1})
is a morphism of right
(n+2)
-angles. Thus (RN3) holds. ∎

This theorem immediately yields the following conclusion.

Corollary 5.2
.

[
AHBT
, Theorem 3.1]

Let
(\mathscr{C},\Sigma,\Phi)
be an
(n+2)
-angulated category.
Then the axiom
(N3)
is a consequence of the axioms
(N1)(c)
and
(N4)
.

Proof.

Since any
(n+2)
-angulated category can be viewed as a right
(n+2)
-angulated category, this follows from Theorem
5.1
. ∎

6
Some equivalent characterizations of axiom (RN4
∗
)

In this section, we will give some equivalent characterizations of axiom (RN4
∗
).

Theorem 6.1
.

If
\Theta
is a collection of
(n+2)
-
\Sigma
-sequences satisfying the axioms
(RN1)
,
(RN2)
and
(RN3)
, then the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

\Theta
satisfies
(RN4
∗
);

(2)

\Theta
satisfies
(RN4-1):

Given the solid part of the commutative diagram

\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{0}}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n-1}}
\textstyle{A_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\scriptstyle{f_{n}}
\textstyle{A_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma f_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{1}}
\textstyle{B_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n-1}}
\textstyle{B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\textstyle{B_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma B_{0}}

with rows in
\Theta
. Then there exist the dotted morphisms such that the above diagram commutes and the
mapping cone

A_{1}\oplus B_{0}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{1}&0\\
f_{1}&b_{0}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{2}\oplus B_{1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{2}&0\\
f_{2}&b_{1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{3}\oplus B_{2}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{3}&0\\
f_{3}&b_{2}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\cdots\hskip 142.26378pt

\cdots\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n}&0\\
f_{n}&b_{n-1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{n+1}\oplus B_{n}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n+1}&0\\
f_{n+1}&b_{n}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{0}\oplus B_{n+1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-\Sigma a_{0}&0\\
\Sigma f_{0}&b_{n+1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{1}\oplus\Sigma B_{0}

belongs to
\Theta
.

Proof.

First, we prove (1) implies (2):
Assume that we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n-1}}

\scriptstyle{a_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\textstyle{A_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma f_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{1}}
\textstyle{B_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n-1}}
\textstyle{B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\textstyle{B_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma B_{0}}

with rows in
\Theta
.
Since
\Theta
closed under direct sums, then the direct sum of three right
(n+2)
-angles

A_{0}\xrightarrow{~1~}A_{0}\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\Sigma A_{0}

0\rightarrow A_{1}\xrightarrow{~1~}A_{1}\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow 0

can be combined to get the following

belongs to
\Theta
. By the commutative diagram below

(6.4)

with rows in
\Theta
. Then there exist morphisms

\displaystyle u:A_{1}\oplus B_{0}\rightarrow A_{2}\oplus B_{1}

\displaystyle v_{2}:A_{2}\oplus B_{1}\rightarrow B_{2}

\displaystyle v_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow B_{i}~~(i=3,\cdots,n)

\displaystyle v_{n+1}:A_{n+1}\rightarrow\Sigma A_{0}\oplus B_{n+1}

such that (
6.4
) commutes and the following
(n+2)
-
\Sigma
-sequence

A_{1}\oplus B_{0}\xrightarrow{~u~}A_{2}\oplus B_{1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{2}&~0\\
v_{2,1}&~v_{2,2}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{3}\oplus B_{2}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{3}&0\\
v_{3}&b_{2}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\cdots\hskip 142.26378pt

\hskip 28.45274pt\cdots\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n}&0\\
v_{n}&b_{n-1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{n+1}\oplus B_{n}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}v_{n+1,1}&~0\\
v_{n+1,2}&~b_{n}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{0}\oplus B_{n+1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-\Sigma a_{0}&0\\
\Sigma f_{0}&b_{n+1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{1}\oplus\Sigma B_{0}

(6.5)

belongs to
\Theta
.

By the commutative diagram (
6.4
), we have the following equalities.

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}v_{2,1}&v_{2,2}\\
\end{matrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{matrix}f_{2}&b_{1},\\
\end{matrix}\right]

\displaystyle v_{3}

\displaystyle=f_{3},

\displaystyle\cdots

\displaystyle v_{n}

\displaystyle=f_{n},

\displaystyle\left[\begin{matrix}v_{n+1,1}\\
v_{n+1,2}\\
\end{matrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[\begin{matrix}-a_{n+1}\\
f_{n+1}\\
\end{matrix}\right].

For
f_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow B_{i}~(i=2,\cdots,n+1)
,
we have the following commutative diagram, that is to say,
f=(f_{0},f_{1},f_{2},\cdots,f_{n+1})
is a morphism of right
(n+2)
-angles

\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{0}}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n-1}}
\textstyle{A_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\scriptstyle{f_{n}}
\textstyle{A_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma f_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{1}}
\textstyle{B_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n-1}}
\textstyle{B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\textstyle{B_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma B_{0}.}

By

u\left[\begin{matrix}1&0&0\\
0&f_{0}&1\\
\end{matrix}\right]=\left[\begin{matrix}-a_{1}&0\\
f_{1}&1\\
\end{matrix}\right]\left[\begin{matrix}1&a_{0}&0\\
0&0&b_{0}\\
\end{matrix}\right],

we get
u=\left[\begin{matrix}-a_{1}&0\\
f_{1}&b_{0}\\
\end{matrix}\right]
.
Substituting
u,v_{i,j}
into (
6.5
), we get
the
(n+2)
-
\Sigma
-sequence

A_{1}\oplus B_{0}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{1}&0\\
f_{1}&b_{0}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{2}\oplus B_{1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{2}&0\\
f_{2}&b_{1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{3}\oplus B_{2}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{3}&0\\
f_{3}&b_{2}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\cdots\hskip 142.26378pt

\cdots\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n}&0\\
f_{n}&b_{n-1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{n+1}\oplus B_{n}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n+1}&0\\
f_{n+1}&b_{n}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{0}\oplus B_{n+1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-\Sigma a_{0}&0\\
\Sigma f_{0}&b_{n+1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{1}\oplus\Sigma B_{0}

belongs to
\Theta
.

Next, we prove (2) implies (1):
This proof process is similar to the necessity of proving Theorem
2.2
, and we have omitted it here. ∎

Remark 6.2
.

The above result
was proved in
[
BT
, Thoerem 4.4]
for an
(n+2)
-angulated category.
Now it has been extended to a right
(n+2)
-angulated category.
However, our proof method differs from
[
BT
, Thoerem 4.4]
as
\Sigma
is not automorphism for
a right
(n+2)
-angulated category.

Theorem 6.3
.

If
\Theta
is a collection of
(n+2)
-
\Sigma
-sequences satisfying the axioms
(RN1)
,
(RN2)
and
(RN3)
, then the following statements are equivalent:

(1)

\Theta
satisfies
(RN4-1);

(2)

\Theta
satisfies
(RN4-2):

Given the solid part of the commutative diagram

\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{2}}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n-1}}
\textstyle{A_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\scriptstyle{f_{n}}
\textstyle{A_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{f_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{1}}
\textstyle{B_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{2}}
\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n-1}}
\textstyle{B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n}\hskip 5.69054pt}
\textstyle{B_{n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b_{n+1}}
\textstyle{\Sigma A_{0}}

with rows in
\Theta
. Then there exist the dotted morphisms
f_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow B_{i}~(i=2,\cdots,n+1)
such that the above diagram commutes and the following
(n+2)
-
\Sigma
-sequence

A_{1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{1}\\
f_{1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{2}\oplus B_{1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{2}&0\\
f_{2}&b_{1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{3}\oplus B_{2}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{3}&0\\
f_{3}&b_{2}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\cdots\hskip 142.26378pt

\cdots\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n}&0\\
f_{n}&b_{n-1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{n+1}\oplus B_{n}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}f_{n+1}&~b_{n}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}B_{n+1}\xrightarrow{\Sigma a_{0}\circ b_{n+1}}\Sigma A_{1}

belongs to
\Theta
.

(6.6)

with rows in
\Theta
. Apply (RN4-1) for diagram (

), there exist
f_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow B_{i}~(i=2,\cdots,n+1)
such that (
6.6
) commutes and the mapping cone

A_{1}\oplus B_{0}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{1}&0\\
f_{1}&b_{0}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{2}\oplus B_{1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{2}&0\\

\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{3}\oplus B_{2}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{3}&0\\
f_{3}&b_{2}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\cdots\hskip 142.26378pt

\cdots\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n}&0\\
f_{n}&b_{n-1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{n+1}\oplus B_{n}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n+1}&0\\

belongs to

\end{smallmatrix}\right]}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 213.18102pt\raise-44.48866pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 213.18102pt\raise-44.48866pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Sigma A_{0}\oplus\Sigma B_{0}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

(6.10)

where the first row of (
6.10
) obtained by the direct sum of

B_{0}\xrightarrow{1}B_{0}\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\Sigma B_{0}

and the first row of diagram (
6.8
), we
apply (RN4-2) for (
6.10
), there exist

f_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow B_{i}~(i=2,\cdots,n),~~\alpha:A_{n+1}\rightarrow\Sigma A_{0}\oplus B_{n+1}

such that the diagram (
6.10
) commutes, where
\alpha=\left[\begin{matrix}-a_{n+1}\\
f_{n+1}\\
\end{matrix}\right]
. i.e.
f=(f_{0},f_{1},f_{2},\cdots,f_{n+1})
is a morphism of right
(n+2)
-angles in (
6.8
),
and the the
(n+2)
-
\Sigma
-sequence

A_{1}\oplus B_{0}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{1}&0\\
f_{1}&b_{0}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{2}\oplus B_{1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{2}&0\\
f_{2}&b_{1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{3}\oplus B_{2}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{3}&0\\
f_{3}&b_{2}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\cdots\hskip 142.26378pt

\cdots\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n}&0\\
f_{n}&b_{n-1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}A_{n+1}\oplus B_{n}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-a_{n+1}&0\\
f_{n+1}&b_{n}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{0}\oplus B_{n+1}\xrightarrow{\left[\begin{smallmatrix}-\Sigma a_{0}&0\\
\Sigma f_{0}&b_{n+1}\\
\end{smallmatrix}\right]}\Sigma A_{1}\oplus\Sigma B_{0}

belongs to
\Theta
. This completes the proof.∎

Remark 6.4
.

Note that Theorem
6.3
was proved in
[
LZ19
, Thoerem 3.1]
for an
(n+2)
-angulated category.
Now it has been extended to a right
(n+2)
-angulated category.
However, our proof method differs from
[
LZ19
, Thoerem 3.1]
as
\Sigma
is not automorphism for
a right
(n+2)
-angulated category.
